# nUIget 2.0 — item tracker

One row per work item of `2026-10-11-nuiget-2.0-execution-plan.md`, in execution order. `RUN.md` reads this file to pick
the next item and updates the row when the item closes. Status values: `open`, `in-progress`, `done`, `blocked`, `skipped`
(owner decision). The `commit` column holds the SHA on `labs/2.0` that closed the item; findings are tracked in
`findings-ledger.md`.

Rows P5.1, P5.3 and P5.4 run before P4 by the P3.6 ruling; the phase review of P4 (`git log labs/2.0-P3-done..HEAD`)
therefore covers them, and the P5 review covers `labs/2.0-P4-done..HEAD`. The phase gates and tags are unchanged.

From the P4.1 ruling: `NodeTransport` passes no `agent` to `http(s).request` (under `http.proxySupport: on` a defined agent bypasses the VS Code proxy, under `override` it is discarded); drop the quarry's `httpsAgent`/`httpAgent` (Node 18 already defaults to TLS 1.2; concurrency is bounded by the feeds' batching, not by `maxSockets`) and rewrite the header comment at quarry `Transport.ts:10-18` (keep-alive does not survive a DIRECT decision under `override`); `shouldUseHttp2` keeps the allow-list and the injected `ProxyState`; a connection-level HTTP/2 failure (DNS, connect, TLS handshake) downgrades the origin for the instance's lifetime, replacing `H2_FAILURE_COOLDOWN_MS`; `NodeTransport.reset()` (class method, not on the `Transport` contract) closes HTTP/2 sessions and clears the downgrade set; redirect re-validation runs while `proxy.isConfigured()` is false and a target that does not resolve is refused with `redirect-blocked` and a message naming the unresolved host; never `rejectUnauthorized: false`, never `undici` or `fetch`. Tests by title: `passes no agent to the request functions so the host proxy patch applies`, `uses HTTP/2 for an allow-listed origin only while the proxy predicate is false`, `a connection-level HTTP/2 failure downgrades the origin for the transport's lifetime`, `reset() restores HTTP/2 for a downgraded origin`, `a redirect target that does not resolve is refused with redirect-blocked`. CHANGELOG `### Added` entry for the proxy behaviour lands here. Done without an L1 consultation (not ⚑; the row's ask "untestable proxy behaviour" did not arise: the P4.1 matrix says what the host does, and the transport's side of it, passing no agent, is tested through an injected request hook). `Transport.ts` copied from the quarry working tree (compiled and linted clean as copied), then changed as the P4.1 ruling's note says: no agent, sticky HTTP/2 downgrade, `reset()`, unresolvable redirect targets refused, DNS of redirect targets under the deadline and the signal, redirect/error/HEAD bodies closed unread. 29 tests in `Transport.test.ts` (gate ≥ 25), the five P4.1 titles among them; 40 runs of 40 green; 10 mutations, 8 caught at once, the error-body one after a test was strengthened, the idle-session downgrade covered twice over. CI's Build & Package job runs `npm run check-types` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, "Type check" step), so a core item with type errors in its tests is caught only after the push. Practice for P1.1a onward: run `npm run check-types` wherever the ritual says `npx tsc --noEmit`; P7.5 may fold that wording into RUN.md/plan §2.2 (owner's documents, not a phase-review edit).
2. Benchmarks are discovered only under `src/test/benchmarks/`: `vitest.config.mts` `benchmark.include` is `['src/test/benchmarks/**/*.bench.ts', 'src/test/benchmarks/benchmark.test.ts']`, and `src/test/benchmarks/run.mjs` selects the derived `backend (bench)` project. P1.3's gate ("`npm run bench` runs the file" for `core-versioning.bench.ts`) therefore needs the bench at `src/test/benchmarks/core-versioning.bench.ts`; a bench under `src/core/**` is silently not run unless P1.3 names `vitest.config.mts` (a config change outside its row, so a stop-and-ask). If the implementing session has another file-writing tool (a multi-edit or notebook-edit variant), a frozen-path write through it is not denied. N26 in `docs/plans/findings-ledger.md:128,332` covers shell forms, not this case. P1.1a confirms the session's tool inventory on first sentinel use and widens N26 if another writing tool exists.

- Ground 1: D6's operative clause is "change only in the items that name them"; the P1.1a row (plan line 207) names `src/protocol/**` and `src/core/contracts.ts`, is titled "Protocol salvage (sentinel required)", and itself specifies two contract edits (`satisfies Record<…, true>` on `REQUEST_TYPES`/`EVENT_TYPES`; `PromiseLike` replaces `Thenable` in `contracts.ts`). P0.8's row defers the `docs:protocol:check` CI step to P1.1a and the P0.4 tracker row expects the `docs:protocol*` scripts from P1.1a, so the plan treats P1.1a as the item that creates the protocol. The asymmetry is not intended; it is a slip in my P1.10 ruling, whose step-7 code sample was written for the `skipRestore: false` path and did not consider the option. Neither ADR-003:26-27 nor ADR-007:21-22 fixes a precedence between "skipped by option" and "cancelled", so this ruling does: a step the user switched off was never going to run, so a cancel cannot have skipped it, and `{ ok: false, skipped: true, error: 'cancelled' }` for that step tells the UI (and `ops/completed`, `messages.ts:190`) that the cancel prevented work it did not. The bulk order is right and `single()` aligns to it. Phase review (`architecture-advisor`, brief `PHASE REVIEW P2`, 2026-10-13), verbatim:

```text
VERDICT: PASS WITH FIXES

The brief describes the tree accurately. Every P2 row has its outputs, named tests and a plausible gate; the salvage gate holds over every P2 directory; no test was deleted, skipped or weakened; the frozen paths changed only in 4982d16 (P2.8: `docs/adr/009-catalog.md`, `docs/adr/README.md`) and e4eea0f (P2.9: `src/protocol/domain.ts`, `src/protocol/messages.ts`, `src/protocol/protocol.test.ts`, `docs/protocol.md`, ADR-002, ADR-006, ADR-008), each under an approval recorded in `docs/plans/STATUS.md` ("CONTRACT CHANGE:" at lines 868 and 1046); `.claude/.contract-change` does not exist. Two defects of the phase would make P3 build on something wrong: the session loses a valid source selection in a race the harness measures (C-state-core-6), and no ok response carries `generation`, which P3.3's `useResource` reads (C-protocol-host-7). Both become fix items before the tag.

BLOCKING:

1. P2.fix-1 — C-state-core-6: `refreshProjectsAndSession` (`src/extension.next.ts:136-149`) lists the sources of the directory of the project selected when the run started (`enabledSourceUrls` → `sourceDirectory(services)`, `handlers.ts:29-36`, read at `:122`) and then calls `session.reconcile(projects, urls)`. `DefaultSessionStore.reconcile` (`src/core/session/SessionStore.ts:100-104`) replaces `knownSourceUrls` and normalizes project and source in one commit, so when the selection moved during the listing (A→B→A) the source of A is judged against B's list and reset to `all` (measured: `scenarios.test.ts:51-83`, revisions 1 to 4, final `all`), and when the selected project vanished the source is judged against the gone directory's or the first workspace folder's list, after which a second run lists the replacement's directory (`src/STATUS.md`, Deviations).

   Answers to question 1. Blocking: yes. Not a two-step reconcile: a first `reconcile(projects, [selectedSource])` would set `knownSourceUrls` to one URL for the whole listing (`SessionStore.ts:102,138`), and a `session/set` that picks another valid source in that window would be reset to `all`; today that narrowing happens only on the failure path, the two-step would make it the normal path of every refresh. The fix predicts the project in the root with the store's own rule and lists that project's directory, then reconciles once. It stays inside `src/extension.next.ts` and `src/extension.next.test.ts`; the harness edit is the one the ledger row names; `src/core/session/**`, `src/core/contracts.ts` and `src/host/**` are untouched.

   Files: `src/extension.next.ts`, `src/extension.next.test.ts`, `src/test/harness/scenarios.test.ts` (the `A→B→A ordering` assertions only), `src/STATUS.md`, `src/test/harness/STATUS.md`, `docs/plans/findings-ledger.md`, `docs/plans/STATUS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the `src/extension.next.ts` row). No frozen file, no sentinel.

   The exact change in `src/extension.next.ts`:
   a. Import the value `ALL_PROJECTS` from `./protocol` (the P2.10 ruling, step 6, allows it) and `firstProject` from `./core/session` (exported, `src/core/session/index.ts:5`).
   b. A local `settledProject(selected: string, projects: Project[]): string`, the rule of `normalizeProject` (`SessionStore.ts:119-131`, ADR-001 lines 19-21): `ALL_PROJECTS` → `projects.length > 1 ? ALL_PROJECTS : firstProject(projects)?.path ?? ''`; a path among `projects` → itself; else `firstProject(projects)?.path ?? ''`. The duplication is deliberate and self-healing: if the prediction ever differs from what the store commits, the `onDidChange` trigger sees a project other than `lastReconciledProject` and runs again. One comment says so.
   c. A local `directoryOf(project: string): string | undefined`: `project && project !== ALL_PROJECTS ? path.dirname(project) : workspaceFolderFor()`; `enabledSourceUrls(directory: string | undefined)` takes it instead of calling `sourceDirectory(services)`; `undefined` is the `not-found` case and takes the existing fallback. The failure fallback (`lastListing` of the same directory, else `[selectedSource]`/`[]`) is unchanged.
   d. `refreshProjectsAndSession`: `const before = session.snapshot.selectedProject; lastReconciledProject = before;` → `projects = await catalog.projects()` (unchanged error path) → `events.projectsChanged.fire(projects)` and `setContext` (unchanged) → `const kept = settledProject(before, projects); lastReconciledProject = kept;` → `const urls = await enabledSourceUrls(directoryOf(kept));` → `const selected = session.snapshot.selectedProject; if (selected !== before && selected !== kept) { return; }` (the selection moved during the listing; the `onDidChange` listener has set `rerun`, because `selected !== lastReconciledProject`) → `await session.reconcile(projects, urls)`. The second half of the condition matters: with the selected project gone, `before` is the gone path and `kept` the replacement, and the snapshot still shows `before` when nothing moved (must reconcile) or `kept` when the user chose exactly the replacement meanwhile (must reconcile too, no rerun will come). Setting `lastReconciledProject = kept` before `reconcile` means the host-origin commit that replaces a project no longer asks for a second run.
   e. `requestRefresh` and the triggers (`extension.next.ts:151-189`) are unchanged.

   Tests, `src/extension.next.test.ts` (the fake's `deferred()` at `:108` and the `dotnet.sources` per `cwd` of `:332` are what both need):
   - `a selection that moves during a refresh is not reconciled against the previous project's sources`: persisted `{ selectedProject: App, selectedSource: privateFeed }`, App's directory lists `[privateFeed, sharedFeed]`, Lib's `[sharedFeed]`; hold the listing for Lib's directory; `services.session.apply({ selectedProject: Lib }, 'sidebar')`; wait until the fake saw the listing for Lib's directory; `services.session.apply({ selectedProject: App }, 'main')`; release; settle. Assert `snapshot.selectedProject === App`, `snapshot.selectedSource === privateFeed`, and that no snapshot with `selectedSource: 'all'` was ever broadcast (collect `session.onDidChange`).
   - `a replaced selection is reconciled against the replacement project's sources`: persisted `{ selectedProject: gonePath, selectedSource: privateFeed }`, App's directory lists `[privateFeed, sharedFeed]`, the gone directory and every other `[sharedFeed]`; after `ready`: `selectedProject === App`, `selectedSource === privateFeed`, the fake saw a listing with `cwd` App's directory and none with the gone directory, and exactly one host-origin `session/changed` for the replacement.

2. P2.fix-2 — C-protocol-host-7: `WebviewHost.dispatch` posts `makeOk(request.id, request.type, result)` (`src/host/WebviewHost.ts:146`); `makeOk` accepts a fourth argument `generation` (`src/protocol/envelope.ts:88-91`) and the ok envelope has `generation?: number` (`:52`), but no handler can supply it because the Catalog's cacheable reads return bare values (`Catalog.ts:94-101,110-143,181-183`) while the coordinator's handle carries the generation (`QueryCoordinator.ts:160,180`). ADR-007:29-31, ADR-008:19-24 and the P3.3 row ("stale discard by id and generation") read a field nobody writes.

   Answers to question 2. Blocking: yes, before P3.3 (its row already says so). The generation travels as a field, not a wrapped return: `RequestContext<T>` (`WebviewHost.ts:35-40`) gains `generation?: number`, documented "set by a handler whose result is a coordinator resource: the generation it was computed for; `WebviewHost` puts it on the ok envelope (ADR-007)"; `dispatch` posts `makeOk(request.id, request.type, result, ctx.generation)`; an error response, a stream chunk and an aborted request never carry it. The `RequestHandler` type and the 43 one-response cases are untouched by this choice.

   Catalog (`src/core/catalog/Catalog.ts`): `export interface CatalogValue<T> { value: T; generation: number }`; `projects`, `sources` and `transitive` return `Promise<CatalogValue<…>>` with `generation: handle.generation` of the handle whose `value` resolved (`queryWithRetry` returns `{ value, generation }` of the attempt that succeeded, so a stale retry reports the new generation); `InstalledQueryResult` gains `generation: number`, the handle's generation of the attempt that resolved, the same for the `{ result }` of a cache hit or joiner, for the `{ result, upgrade }` of a running evaluation, and for the provisional that `upgrade` falls back to when the evaluation is superseded (that fallback is then below the generation the event lists, which is exactly what the webview must discard; note on the P6.2 row that this fallback, `Catalog.ts:132`, becomes the first real producer of such a response). A cache hit reports `entry.generation` (equal to `valueGeneration` by the hit condition, `QueryCoordinator.ts:158-160`); a joiner reports the computation's generation; both surfaces therefore see the same number for the same `(key, generation)`. The fan-outs `allProjectsInstalled` and `allProjectsTransitive` unwrap `.value` internally (`Catalog.ts:205,214,246,253`) and report no generation: they are not resources (ADR-009 decision 1).

   Handlers that set `ctx.generation` (nine): `projects/list`, `installed/get`, `transitive/get`, `sources/list`, `sources/refresh`, and `sources/add`, `sources/remove`, `sources/enable`, `sources/disable` (their `sources` is a fresh read of `sources:<dir>` after the `sources` invalidation in `afterSourceMutation`, `handlers.ts:307-312`, so it is "a result computed for a generation" in the sense of ADR-007:29-31; without it a webview that shows sources through `useResource` could not order the mutation's answer against the event the mutation itself fired). Not set: `allProjects/*`, `updates/get` (not yet available), `session/*`, `layout/*`, `ops/*`, `ui/*`, `refresh/all`, `perf/mark`. Call sites that unwrap `.value`: `extension.next.ts:123,141`, `SidebarHost.ts:80,88,156,229`, `handlers.ts:254,256,273,299,309-310,322,338`, `harness.ts:343`, and `handlers.test.ts:98` (the fake catalog returns the new shapes). No frozen file changes: `envelope.ts`, `messages.ts`, `docs/protocol.md`, `contracts.ts`, the ADRs stay; `npm run docs:protocol:check` must still exit 0.

   Tests:
   - `src/host/WebviewHost.test.ts`: `a generation set on the context reaches the ok envelope` (handler sets `ctx.generation = 3`, posted envelope `{ ok: true, generation: 3 }`), `an error response and a stream chunk carry no generation`.
   - `src/host/handlers.test.ts`: extend `it.each(REQUEST_TYPES)('%s: one request → exactly one terminal response')` (`:186`) so that for exactly the nine types above the ok envelope has `typeof generation === 'number'` and for every other type `'generation' in envelope` is false; the fake catalog answers with the new shapes.
   - `src/core/catalog/Catalog.test.ts`: `a cache hit and a joiner report the computation's generation` (two `projects()` calls during one computation and a third after it: all three `generation` equal `coordinator.generationOf(resourceKeys.projects())`), `a stale retry reports the generation it finally resolved for`, `installed reports the handle's generation with and without an upgrade`.
   - `src/test/harness/scenarios.test.ts`, new scenario — the ledger's title "late response for an invalidated key is discarded by generation" cannot be satisfied by this host: ADR-002 invariants 2 and 3 make the coordinator reject a result an invalidation overtook, `queryWithRetry` turns the third stale into an error, and the host posts in order, so no ok response below the listed generation exists before P6.2; the scenario is named for what it proves: `generation on responses: every resource response carries the generation the latest invalidation lists for its key, on both surfaces`. Both surfaces resolved and showing A: every `installed/get` ok response for A on both surfaces carries the same `generation`, every `projects/list` response the same for `projects`; `refresh('main')`; the `resources/invalidated` event lists `installed:A` and `projects` with new generations; after `expectState`, every response delivered after the event carries exactly the listed generation, on both surfaces; `expectOneResponsePerRequest`. Correct the ledger row's title when closing it.
   - `FakeWebview.ts` rule 7 (header `:18`, code `:229-243`) becomes: "An ok response of the `projects` or `installed` slot carries `generation`; it is applied only when that generation is not below the one the latest `resources/invalidated` event listed for its key (`projects`, `installed:<path>`); a lower one is recorded and not applied. Rule 4 (latest request id wins) is unchanged; chunks are never compared." The model keeps the last listed generation per key from `onInvalidated`.

   Docs: ARCHITECTURE "Reading through the Catalog" (the four reads return `{ value, generation }`; the nine handlers put it on the envelope; fan-outs do not) and the harness paragraph's rule 7 (`ARCHITECTURE.md:191-192`); the `src/host/**` row; `src/core/catalog/STATUS.md`, `src/host/STATUS.md`, `src/test/harness/STATUS.md` (drop "No scenario asserts a response `generation`"); ledger C-protocol-host-7 → closed with the corrected scenario title; P3.3 row note → "fixed in P2.fix-2"; P6.2 row note as above; CHANGELOG: amend the Added bullet "2.0 webview hosts" with "and every response that is a cached resource carries the generation it was computed for".

   VERIFY:

Question 4, P2.9 CPM memo: `createWorkflowDeps` (`src/host/services.ts:54-87`) clears the per-project answer only when `onDidChange` reports `active === 0`; `DefaultOperationQueue.finish` starts the next waiting job before it fires the change (`src/core/ops/OperationQueue.ts:195-203`), so between consecutive queued operations `active` is 1 and the answer spans the whole busy stretch, not one operation. Accepted as the memo the P1 review asked for, with this correction scheduled: a props edit that switches CPM on while the queue is busy is seen by the watcher only after idle, so a queued add can run with `isCpm: false`; today N51's literal masks it. P6.3, which rewrites `admitEdit` as `ProjectMutator.canMutate`, clears the memo on every `onDidChange` event (or keys it by ticket) and adds `the CPM answer is asked again for the next operation` to `handlers.test.ts`.

### P1.1b — checkpoint before starting (2026-10-12)

Brief: plan §2.4 form, item P1.1b, on `labs/2.0` @ `ac64e72` with all gates green and no edit made. It listed
the twelve contract changes and seven ADR amendments of the plan row, marked the ones whose shape the row does
not give (3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 11, ADR-005, the form of the amendments), pasted the quarry lines it relied on, and
asked what exactly is to be declared in P1.1b. Answer:

```text
DECISION: Option 1. Every OPEN point is ruled below; nothing in the row is the owner's. Rulings on the open points: 3 = `ProjectMutator` with the two query members P6.3 names (`canMutate`, `previewCpmImpact`) over named request/decision types, no `apply`; 4 = `resourceKeys.sources(projectDir)` with a required argument; 5 = literal request name `'readme/image'` placed in the Package details group, payload `{ url: string; sourceUrl?: string }`, result `{ url: string; dataUri: string | null }`; 7 = `generation?: number` on the ok variant of `ResponseEnvelope` only (not on result types, not on `StreamEnvelope`), with an optional fourth parameter on `makeOk`; 8 = the naming rule is recorded in ADR-007 and its consequences are exactly: the three `source?: string` payload fields → `sourceUrl?`, the two `resourceKeys` parameters → `sourceUrl`, `search/query.sources?: string[]` → `sourceUrls?`; `selectedSource`, `search/autocomplete.sources`, `removedUrl`, `disabledUrl`, `NuGetSource.url`, `FailedSource.url` keep their names; 9 = export the union as `CapabilityProvenance`; 11 = no contract edit in P1.1b, the P1.6 row carries `AddPackageOptions.isCpm`; ADR-005 = strike `undici`, make the proxy sentence equal to the `Transport` comment at `src/core/contracts.ts:238-242`, name `ProjectExecutionContext` as the context contract, leave the configuration matrix and the vendoring decision to P4.1; amendments are written inline in the form ADR-000 used in P0.9, not as appended sections; the N42 order (tracker, sentinel, protocol note) is right.

WHY: I verified every path:line in the brief against `labs/2.0` @ ac64e72 and all hold: `src/core/contracts.ts:98` has `rejected: (reason: string) => T`, `:173-174` says "merged over the sanitized base env", `:185-192` still declares `nounFirstSyntax`, `:307-314` declares `CpmImpactPreview`; `src/protocol/domain.ts:69` lacks `sdk-too-old` and `:225` is `sources: (): ResourceKey => 'sources'`; `src/protocol/messages.ts:131` lacks `sourcesUnavailable` while `:121` and `:127` already carry it as optional; `src/protocol/envelope.ts:48-50` is the two-variant union; ADR-005 says "via `https.Agent`/`undici` proxy agent" and ADR-006 says "`readme/image` lazily". Three facts the brief did not carry decide the open points: (i) ADR-000's amendment of P0.9 (commit 583ef41) is inline, a header line `Status: accepted · Date: 2026-10-10 · Amended: 2026-10-12 (plan item P0.9: …)`, a Context paragraph and rewritten bullets, so appended sections would give the ADR set two forms and force readers to compute the effective decision from two texts; (ii) `tseslint.configs.recommended` is on (`eslint.config.mjs:92`) and includes `no-empty-object-type`, so option (c) for the mutator (an empty interface) is lint-red, and P6.3's row names core/project and host but not `contracts.ts`, so deferring members guarantees an unplanned contract-change item later; (iii) `domain.ts:260` already has `OperationKind = 'install' | 'update' | 'remove' | 'restore'` and the house shape for a failed result is `{ ok: false; error }` (`contracts.ts:257` `SourceMutationResult`, `domain.ts:272` `StepOutcome`, P1.10's refusal `{ ok:false, error:'…' }`), so the mutator reuses both instead of inventing `add` and `reason`. For 4, ADR-005 resolves sources per project directory and `SourceProvider.listSources(projectDir)` (`contracts.ts:264`) is per directory, so a bare `'sources'` key has no meaning and an optional argument would create two keys for one resource. For 5, the name `readme/image` appears in ADR-006 and in three plan rows (P1.1b, P4.4, P5.7) that are executed literally and that P7.5's drift script may grep; results echoing a request field is the house pattern (`package/versions` echoes `packageId`, `updates/get` echoes `projectPath`, `search/query` echoes `query`); `sourceUrl?` lets the host confine credentials to the README's own feed (ADR-006 "same host-side fetch … auth"), and a `null` data URI is ADR-006's "shown as links when the fetch fails". For 7, ADR-002 says "Request ids correlate RPC; generations establish freshness", which is a transport-level property, one declaration on the envelope serves every resource request without enumerating which results are resources, the guard `isResponseEnvelope` needs no change for an optional field, and streams need no generation because ADR-002 invariant 2 terminates a request whose generation is superseded with `StaleQueryError`, so a chunk never outlives its generation and chunks are dropped by request id alone. For 8, ADR-007's Context already names "the same concept had different field names per surface" as the problem it solves, so that is where the rule belongs; `ProjectExecutionContext.sourceUrls` (`contracts.ts:290`) and `BulkPackageRef.sourceUrl` (`messages.ts:42`) are the precedent. For 11, `AddPackageOptions` is an argv-builder option type internal to `src/core/cli`, not a cross-module contract, and moving it would make P1.6's copy of `commands.ts` collide with a declaration in `contracts.ts`, forcing a delete-and-reimport; that loses work if P1.6 is interrupted. For ADR-005, the P4.1 row (plan line 257) owns "supported configuration and matrix; decide on vendoring a proxy agent" and D6 lists P4.1 as the ADR-005 contract item, so P1.1b writes only what is already decided and already stated in `contracts.ts:238-242`.

### P1.7 — stop-and-ask: contract note on reconcile (2026-10-12)

Brief: the item asks for a "contract note that the host calls `reconcile` on project change", while the frozen
`src/core/contracts.ts:66` said `reconcile` is "called by the coordinator"; ADR-001, the P2.10 row and both
call sites in the quarry say the host calls it. P1.7 is not marked as a sentinel item. Question: where does the
note go, with the exact text, and how should `scopeMatchesKey` take the platform? Answer:

```text
DECISION: Option 3, narrowed: the JSDoc at `src/core/contracts.ts:66` is replaced under this answer as the recorded advisor approval (sentinel content `P1.7`, no signature change, no other line of the file), and `DefaultSessionStore.reconcile` gets a two-sentence implementation JSDoc that points at the contract instead of repeating the caller rule. DECISION: (1) Option 1a, confined to `src/core/contracts.ts`: `InvalidationEvent.keys` becomes `Array<{ key: ResourceKey; generation: number }>`, the event's `generation` stays and is documented as a per-event sequence number that is not comparable with a key's generation; the per-key value is recorded at bump time (last bump in a coalesced tick wins, which equals the flush-time value). The protocol event `resources/invalidated`, ADR-002 and ADR-008 are not touched in P1.8; the protocol side goes to the item that first emits the event (P2.9/P2.10) under its own approval, with tracker notes written now. (2) A `carried: Set<Subscription>` on `Computation` — 2b's data structure with 2a's lifetime: a stale computation's unreleased `active` handles are moved into `next.carried` as a provisional keep-alive that ends when a consumer joins `next`, when `next` settles, at the next invalidation (carried handles never count for a further carry-over) or on an explicit `release()`, whichever comes first; liveness everywhere is `active.size + carried.size`. No ADR edit is needed for it. (3) The seven titles are literal `it()` titles, exactly as written in the row; extra tests are allowed and two are named below. The quarry working tree (with its uncommitted edits) is the base for the copy; "apply uncommitted refactor" and D3 mean the same thing.

WHY: D6 (plan line 50) and the recorded P1.1b rulings (`docs/plans/STATUS.md:33`, `:293`, `:310`, `:366`; `src/core/session/STATUS.md:136`) approve one thing for P1.8: the `InvalidationEvent` member of `src/core/contracts.ts`. The P1.8 row (plan line 215) names `InvalidationEvent.keys` and no protocol or ADR file, its gate is "tests green; eslint 0; tsc 0" with no `docs:protocol:check`, nothing in the tree consumes the event yet (grep above), and the advisor's own tiebreak is the narrowest contract and the least work lost: the first emitter (P2.9 `WebviewHost`, P2.10 "watcher → `resources/invalidated`", plan lines 233-234) and the first consumer (P3.3, line 246) settle the protocol shape with `docs:protocol:check` as their gate. Neither ADR describes the core member, so the contracts edit creates no ADR mismatch; the existing mismatch (ADR-002:56 broadcasts one global `generation`, ADR-008:21 compares per key) predates P1.8 and travels as tracker notes. 1a keeps `generation` because removing it would also change the protocol event and ADR-002:56; 1c leaves `keys` as it is, which is not what the row says ("`keys` carries"). For (2) the brief's reading of the leak is the right one: a carried subscription is already rejected, is never in `next.attached`, and in the quarry stays in `next.active` until the consumer releases a handle that already failed, so a consumer that re-queries without releasing (a) blocks the abort of `next` when its new handle is released, (b) is carried again on every later invalidation and starts a recompute for nobody, and (c) is held for the entry's lifetime. The carried set bounds that interest to one generation and to the first of four definite events, which keeps ADR-002 invariant 2 literally true ("recomputes if any subscriber remains": the carry test stays `current.active.size > 0`) and invariant 4 intact (joining replaces the provisional keep-alives, so "the last subscriber leaves" means the real ones). 2c would delete behaviour ADR-002 and the class comment describe for no gain in safety.

export interface InvalidationEvent {

5. The `carryOver` fix. Types:
```ts
interface Subscription {
    released: boolean;
    deferred: Deferred;
    onChunk?: ChunkListener;
    /** Computation this subscription currently keeps alive (re-pointed when carried over). */
    owner: Computation;
}

interface Computation {
    key: ResourceKey;
    generation: number;
    controller: AbortController;
    compute: ComputeFn;
    /** Handles created for this computation; they receive its value and chunks. */
    attached: Set<Subscription>;
    /** Unreleased handles among `attached`. */
    active: Set<Subscription>;
    /**
     * Unreleased handles of the stale predecessor, already rejected with `StaleQueryError`. They keep this
     * recompute alive only until a consumer joins it, it settles or the next invalidation, and are never
     * carried a second time; they do not receive its value.
     */
    carried: Set<Subscription>;
    settled: boolean;
}
```
(keep the quarry's names for `Deferred`, `ChunkListener`, `ComputeFn`). `create()` initialises `carried: new Set()`. Two module-level helpers:
```ts
function isAlive(computation: Computation): boolean {
    return computation.active.size > 0 || computation.carried.size > 0;
}

/** End the provisional keep-alive of carried-over handles; a later `release()` of such a handle is a no-op. */
function dropCarried(computation: Computation): void {
    for (const subscription of computation.carried) {
        subscription.released = true;
    }
    computation.carried.clear();
}
```
`invalidate()`, per matching entry (the carry test stays `current.active.size > 0`; carried handles never count):
```ts
entry.generation++;
affected.set(key, entry.generation);
const current = entry.current;
if (!current) {
    continue;
}
entry.current = undefined;
current.controller.abort();
const stale = new StaleQueryError(key, current.generation);
for (const subscription of current.attached) {
    subscription.deferred.reject(stale);
}
if (!current.settled && current.active.size > 0) {
    this.carryOver(entry, current);
}
dropCarried(current);
```
`carryOver`:
```ts
/**
 * Start the recompute for the new generation right away and let the stale computation's unreleased handles
 * keep it alive provisionally, so a consumer that re-`query()`s on `StaleQueryError` joins work already in
 * flight. Only `active` handles are carried; a recompute kept alive solely by carried handles is not carried
 * again (see `invalidate`). The `compute` call is deferred by one microtask so a synchronous burst of
 * invalidations starts at most one recompute.
 */
private carryOver(entry: Entry, stale: Computation): void {
    const next = this.create(entry, stale.key, stale.compute);
    for (const subscription of stale.active) {
        subscription.owner = next;
        next.carried.add(subscription);
    }
    stale.active.clear();
    queueMicrotask(() => this.run(entry, next));
}
```
`query()`: in the branch that joins `entry.current`, directly after `current.attached.add(subscription); current.active.add(subscription);` add `dropCarried(current);` (a real subscriber replaces the provisional ones). `settle()`: first statement `dropCarried(computation);`, before the `entry.current !== computation` check; the rest of `settle` is unchanged. `release()`:
```ts
private release(entry: Entry, subscription: Subscription): void {
    if (subscription.released) {
        return;
    }
    subscription.released = true;
    const owner = subscription.owner;
    owner.active.delete(subscription);
    owner.carried.delete(subscription);
    if (isAlive(owner)) {
        return;
    }
    queueMicrotask(() => {
        if (isAlive(owner) || this.disposed) {
            return;
        }
        owner.controller.abort();
        if (!owner.settled && entry.current === owner) {
            entry.current = undefined;
        }
    });
}
```
Update the class comment's sentence about carry-over to: "if any of those handles is still subscribed — starts the recompute for the new generation right away, so a consumer that re-`query()`s on `StaleQueryError` joins work already in flight instead of starting over; the old handles keep that recompute alive only until a consumer joins it, it settles or the next invalidation, and are never carried twice."

Add two for the leak: `carry-over: a joining subscriber replaces the carried keep-alives` (query A; invalidate while A's compute is pending; A's `value` rejects with `StaleQueryError`; A re-queries without releasing the old handle and gets a handle for generation 1; the second `compute` call was made and the new handle joined it, so `compute` was called exactly twice; release the new handle only; after a flush the second call's `ctx.signal.aborted` is `true` — the quarry leaves it `false`) and `carry-over: a recompute kept alive only by carried handles is not carried again` (query A; two synchronous `invalidate` calls while A's compute is pending; after a flush `compute` was called exactly once more at most — exactly once in total with the step-3 guard — and no second call was made for generation 1; then a fresh query computes generation 2 and `generationOf(key)` is 2). `invalidate coalesces into one event` asserts the merged scope, one event, `event.generation === 2`, and `event.keys` equal (as a set) to the bumped keys with their current generations, including a key bumped twice in the same tick reporting its final generation. `dispose rejects pending` pins the error class the quarry's `dispose()` actually uses (read it; do not guess) and asserts no `onDidInvalidate` event fires for an `invalidate` after `dispose`. Use deferred promises and a `flushMicrotasks(rounds = 8)` helper (a loop of `await Promise.resolve()`), never timers. Ordering facts to rely on: a rejected handle's `catch` reaction runs before the carried `run` microtask (the reject is synchronous inside `invalidate`, `queueMicrotask(run)` is enqueued after it), so a re-`query()` inside the handler joins `next` before `compute` runs — that is the test shape for "joins work already in flight"; the abort after a last `release()` lands one microtask after `run`, so when a consumer releases a stale handle without re-querying, assert `signal.aborted` after a flush, not the call count; the event flush and the carried `run` are each one microtask after `invalidate()`.

DECISION: Approve P2.8 as a contract item for two frozen files only (`docs/adr/009-catalog.md`, new, and one row in `docs/adr/README.md`), sentinel content `P2.8`. Confirm readings A (with the error shape below), B, E, G, H (plus `allProjectsTransitive`) and I (extend the barrel). Correct D: `installed()` does not resolve with a bare `InstalledResult` and the authoritative result does not travel through `emit`; it resolves with `{ result, upgrade? }` where `upgrade` is the authoritative promise, because the envelope orders every chunk before the terminal response. Correct F: no typed error and no `defaultCwd`/`sourceCwd` deps; `sources(projectDir)` takes a required directory and the host decides which one. Correct C where its decision (1) overclaims and (5) describes what P2.8 does not build; the ADR text is under DO 3. Nothing here is the owner's.

WHY: The brief describes the tree accurately: the raw copy fails on `../feeds/types` (`Catalog.ts:26`), on the two free functions that are `AssetsReader` methods since P2.2 (`:28` vs `assets.ts:171,199`), on `resourceKeys.sources()` without its argument (`:103` vs `domain.ts:227`), reads `process.cwd()` at `:101` and `:352`, and fabricates the evaluator context at `:150-156` (`configFiles: []`, `revision: ''`), while `ProjectContextProvider.get` (`context.ts:37-60`) answers `{ context, unsupported? }` with the real chain and revision; no `src/core/feeds`, no `evaluator.ts`, no ADR-009 exist. Three facts decide D: `src/protocol/envelope.ts:4-8` and `messages.ts:4-6` make the response terminal and chunks precede it, so a host that answers with the provisional cannot deliver the authoritative afterwards; `installed/get` (`messages.ts:120`) has only a `metadata` chunk, so the chunk that carries an `InstalledResult` is a protocol change that belongs to the item that adds the evaluator (P6.2, a D6 contract item), not to P2.8; and the coordinator's cache hit (`QueryCoordinator.ts:157-160`) returns a handle attached to no computation, which receives no chunk, so whatever `compute` returns is what every later reader gets, and that must be the authoritative result or every reader after the evaluation would see the provisional forever. ADR-002:31-32 says `search/query` has no key, which corrects decision (1); `ProjectContextProvider.get` rejects when the source provider rejects (`context.ts:32-35,39-43`, by P2.5's ruling), which forces decision (4) to say that `installed()` degrades instead of propagating. `AssetsReader` is mtime-keyed (`assets.ts:65-71`) and the context provider holds no cache, so `invalidate` has exactly two calls to make in P2.8. RUN.md §4 and plan §3 step 2 forbid a stub, and the row's own words ("feed-dependent methods throw `not-yet-available` with a `// REMOVAL GATE: P4.9` comment") are the sanctioned alternative; only `resolvedSources` has a feeds type in its signature (`:109`, `ResolvedSource[]`), every other feed method is expressible in domain types. P2.5's note (`src/core/project/STATUS.md:287-289`) left the barrel to "the first importer … in its own item", and P2.10's row requires the root to import only what P2 exports from `./core/project`, so P2.8 extends `src/core/project/index.ts` under this approval.

```text
# ADR-009: Catalog, the use-case layer over the query coordinator

Status: accepted · Date: 2026-10-12

## Context

Two surfaces ask the same questions about the same projects, and in 1.x each panel composed the services itself (`NuGetPanel.ts`, `NuGetSidebarPanel.ts`): both computed updates for the same project with their own refresh verbs and caches. ADR-002 gives one computation per resource and generation; ADR-004 and ADR-005 give the project model and its execution context. Missing was the one place where a read request becomes a coordinator query over those modules, so that handlers stay thin and never compose core modules themselves. The quarry's `Catalog.ts` had that role but blocked on the evaluator, fabricated the execution context and took directories from `process.cwd()` (findings C-project-catalog-2, -3 and -6).

## Decision

1. Hosts read through the `Catalog` (`src/core/catalog`) and nowhere else. The Catalog has one method per read request of `RequestMap` (`src/protocol/messages.ts`) plus the two lookups `PackageWorkflows` needs (`packageDependencies`, `projectDependencies`). Every cacheable read is exactly one `QueryCoordinator.query` under the key that `resourceKeys` builds for that request, so both surfaces share one computation per generation. Reads that ADR-002 leaves without a key (`search/query`, `search/autocomplete`, `package/readme`, `transitive/metadata`) run directly. The `allProjects/*` methods are fan-outs over the per-project resources and are not coordinator resources themselves: a cache hit delivers the final value and no chunk, and the payload of an all-projects read is its chunks; the `allProjects:*` keys of `resourceKeys` stay reserved.
2. Streams are `emit` callbacks. The chunk types of the all-projects methods are the protocol's `RequestChunk<…>` types (type-only import), so the host forwards them unchanged. The coordinator caches what the compute function returns; a cache hit and a late joiner receive that value and no chunk, so the returned value is complete on its own and chunks only precede it.
3. `installed(projectPath)` resolves as soon as the provisional result exists, with `{ result, upgrade? }`. `upgrade` is present only when an evaluation has started; it resolves with the authoritative result, or with the provisional again when the evaluation fails or the generation is superseded, and it never rejects. The coordinator caches the authoritative result when there is one, so a cache hit and a joiner during the evaluation receive `{ result: authoritative }` without `upgrade`. The host streams the provisional as a chunk and sends the authoritative as the terminal response (ADR-007: chunks precede the response); the chunk kind that carries it is added to `installed/get` by the item that adds the evaluator (P6.2), which also decides when an evaluation is needed. An evaluation never runs for an `unsupported` project.
4. Every project-scoped read builds its `ProjectExecutionContext` through `ProjectContextProvider` (ADR-005), and nothing in `src/core/catalog` reads `process.cwd()`; a read that needs a directory without a project takes it from the caller (`sources(projectDir)`), and the host decides which directory that is. A context whose SDK is below 10 is reported as `InstalledResult.unsupported = { reason: 'sdk-too-old', message: 'requires .NET SDK 10 or newer' }` on the provisional result with its packages kept, because the project stays readable (D13); an `unsupported` set by the inspector is kept as it is. When the context cannot be built, the provisional result stands without evaluation and the failure is logged: a source listing that fails does not hide the package list.
5. Sources, outbound policy and credentials belong to a project context (ADR-005). A Catalog method resolves them from the context of the project the read is for, never from a workspace-wide default; the feed-dependent methods that consume them arrive with P4.9.
6. `invalidate(scope, reason)` is the single entry for invalidation. It always forwards to `QueryCoordinator.invalidate`; for the scopes `sources` and `workspace` it first clears the per-directory source listings (`SourceProvider.invalidate`), and P4.9 adds the service-index caches and `IconResolver.reset()` at the same place. Caches that check their inputs themselves (the mtime-keyed `AssetsReader`) are not cleared here.
7. A query handle is released when the caller's `AbortSignal` aborts; a call without a signal releases its handle when the value settles. A handle that rejects with `StaleQueryError` is released and the query is asked again, at most three times; the third `StaleQueryError` is thrown.
8. Breadth is frozen until P4 (plan §7): no use case is added to the Catalog without amending this record. The feed-dependent methods (`updates`, `allProjectsUpdates`, `transitiveMetadata`, `search`, `quickSearch`, `versions`, `metadata`, `readme`) are declared with domain-typed signatures and throw `NotYetAvailableError` (`code: 'not-yet-available'`) before creating any query handle until P4.9 wires the feeds; the host maps that error to the protocol error code `unsupported`.

## Consequences

- Handlers are thin: validate the payload, call one Catalog method, forward chunks, send one terminal response.
- Mutations never go through the Catalog: they are `PackageWorkflows` on the `OperationQueue` (ADR-003). The Catalog learns of them through `invalidate`.
- `search/query` carries `sourcesUnavailable` from the Catalog's `search` result (an unreachable source is not an empty result).
```

Delete `serviceIndex`, `search`, `updates`, `enricher`, `metadata`, `icons`, `defaultCwd`, `sourceCwd` and the `../feeds/types` import.

The ninth `// REMOVAL GATE: P4.9` comment goes into `invalidate()` at the line where `serviceIndex.clearCaches()` and `icons.reset()` return.

Tracker note on the P2.9 row: the host answers `sources/list` and `sources/refresh` with `failed: []` under a `// REMOVAL GATE: P4.9` comment, and maps `NotYetAvailableError` to the protocol error code `unsupported` by its `code` field, not its message.

DECISION: Approve P2.9 as a contract-change item, sentinel `.claude/.contract-change` with content exactly `P2.9`, for eight frozen files and nothing else: `src/protocol/messages.ts` (two lines: the `ResourceKey` import and the `resources/invalidated` member), `src/protocol/domain.ts` (the `ExtensionConfig` interface and the N43 comment at line 21), `src/protocol/protocol.test.ts` (it is under `src/protocol/**`, so the hook freezes it), `docs/protocol.md` (regenerated), `docs/adr/002-query-coordinator.md`, `docs/adr/008-webview-architecture.md` and `docs/adr/006-icons-and-csp.md` (one header fragment and one bullet each). `src/core/contracts.ts` is not edited: its line 348 ("`HostServices.mutator` is absent until then (P2.9)") names the member the quarry's `services.ts:24` declares and P6.3 restores, and it stays true after this item. Readings A (with the additions above), C, D and G are confirmed with the corrections below; B is corrected on three members; E is answered from the file; F gets the exact string. Nothing here is the owner's: the plan row (line 233) names every file, D6 allows an advisor-approved contract item, and the P1.8 ruling (`docs/plans/STATUS.md:723`) already assigned the protocol event to P2.9.

WHY: The brief describes the tree accurately: `npx tsc --noEmit` on the byte-identical copy reports 36 errors, 33 in `handlers.ts` (`:120` wraps an already-shaped chunk and expects a bare `InstalledResult`; `:140,147,154` read origin fields off `{ kind: 'project', chunk }`; `:161-168` use `sources`/`source` where ADR-007's naming rule gives `sourceUrls`/`sourceUrl`; `:170,177,178,185,309` call `resolvedSources`/`sourceHealth`/`resetSourceHealth`, which P2.8 dropped until P4.9; `:176,192,205,221` pass `string | undefined` to `catalog.sources(projectDir)`), 2 in `SidebarHost.ts` (`:86,108`, the same two causes) and 1 in `services.ts:11` (`../core/project/mutator`, a P6.3 module). D6 (plan line 50) and the recorded P1.8 ruling make P2.9 the item for the protocol event, and ADR-009 decision 4 ("the host decides which directory") plus ADR-005:19-20 ("sources are resolved per project directory") decide the `sources/list` directory: the directory of the selected project, not its workspace folder as the quarry does. ADR-007 ("exactly one terminal response (ok or error) per id") makes an error envelope a terminal response, so a gated read answering `unsupported` counts toward the gate, and ADR-009 decision 8 fixes that mapping. N9 is live in the quarry and inside this item's files: `WebviewHost.detach()` (`:76-83`) aborts every in-flight controller and `awaitTicket` (`:40`) turns any abort into `ticket.cancel()`, so closing the sidebar cancels the user's queued install; Node 18.15 has `AbortController.abort(reason)` and `signal.reason` (since 17.2), so the fix stays inside D10. The plan row says "CSP per ADR-006 (+`font-src`, nonce)" while ADR-006 still reads `script-src ${cspSource}` and names no `font-src`; approving the code without the ADR sentence would create the contract/ADR mismatch that plan §2.3 makes a stop-and-ask for the next reader, so ADR-006 is on the sentinel list. Plan §3 step 5 forbids `Thenable` outside `contracts.ts`; `WebviewHost.ts:45` has one.

Replace the bullet at lines 56-59 with:

  `InvalidationEvent` forwarded unchanged: `generation` is the event's sequence number, and `keys`
  lists every key the coordinator has seen that `scope` reached, each with its generation after the
  invalidation. Each webview re-requests only the resources it currently displays. Both surfaces
  requesting the same key share one computation. A webview compares the `generation` of an ok
  response envelope with the generation listed for the same key in the latest event, never with the
  event's own `generation` (ADR-007, ADR-008).

  `request` exposes the `generation` of the ok response, so `useResource` can discard a result whose
  generation is below the one listed for its key in the latest `resources/invalidated` event

8. `src/host/services.ts`. `HostServices` after P2.1 to P2.8:
```ts
export interface HostServices {
    readonly context: vscode.ExtensionContext;
    readonly logger: Logger;
    readonly session: SessionStore;
    readonly catalog: Catalog;
    readonly queue: OperationQueue;
    readonly workflows: PackageWorkflows;
    readonly sources: SourceProvider;
    /** Fired after any operation completes (either surface); hosts forward it as `ops/completed`. */
    readonly onOperationCompleted: Event<OperationOutcome | BulkOutcome>;
    /** `queue.onProgress`; hosts forward it as `ops/progress`. */
    readonly onOperationProgress: Event<OperationProgress>;
    readonly onConfigChanged: Event<ExtensionConfig>;
    readonly onProjectsChanged: Event<Project[]>;
    invalidate(scope: InvalidationScope, reason: InvalidationReason): void;
    announceOperation(outcome: OperationOutcome | BulkOutcome): void;
    config(): ExtensionConfig;
    /** Workspace folder (fsPath) containing `projectPath`, or the first folder when none is given; `undefined` without a workspace. */
    workspaceFolderFor(projectPath?: string): string | undefined;
}
```
`Event<T>` is the core type from `../core/contracts` (what `Emitter.event` and `queue.onProgress` already are). Dropped: `mutator` (P6.3 restores it under that name) and `output` (no file under `src/host` reads it; the composition root owns the channel). `readExtensionConfig()` stays in this file and is the one place that reads `vscode.workspace.getConfiguration('nuiget')`: remove `recentSearchesLimit`, add `enablePerformanceLogging: config.get<boolean>('enablePerformanceLogging', false)`; keep the `'fullsearch'` mapping (see N57 in step 16). `createNonce()` becomes `return randomBytes(16).toString('base64');` with `import { randomBytes } from 'crypto';`; delete the alphabet loop. The CSP array becomes `"default-src 'none'"`, `` `img-src ${webview.cspSource} data:` ``, `` `style-src ${webview.cspSource}` ``, `` `font-src ${webview.cspSource}` ``, `` `script-src 'nonce-${nonce}'` ``, `"connect-src 'none'"` joined by `'; '`, and the meta tag is `content="${csp}"` without the trailing `;`. Add the workflow-deps factory here (P2.10 calls it):
```ts
export function createWorkflowDeps(deps: { runner: DotnetRunner; catalog: Catalog; inspector: ProjectInspector; queue: Pick<OperationQueue, 'onDidChange'>; logger?: Logger; onCommand?: WorkflowDeps['onCommand'] }): WorkflowDeps
```
with `packageDependencies: p => deps.catalog.packageDependencies(p)`, `projectDependencies: ps => deps.catalog.projectDependencies(ps)`, and `isCpmProject` memoized in a `Map<string, Promise<boolean>>` keyed by `normalizeProjectPath(projectPath)` (`src/core/util/paths`): on a miss it stores `deps.inspector.inspect(projectPath).then(result => { if (result.unsupported) { throw new Error(result.unsupported.message); } return result.isCpm; })`, deletes the entry again when that promise rejects (a failed read is not memoized), and clears the whole map from `deps.queue.onDidChange` whenever `active === 0`, so the memo lives for one running operation. The throw for `unsupported` is deliberate: `WorkflowDeps.isCpmProject` (`PackageWorkflows.ts:30`) says a rejection refuses the edit, and it is the one place the host keeps an `install`/`update` off a packages.config or non-SDK-style project (`admitEdit` gates only on SDK and CPM). Keep `HostEvents`, `loggerFor`, `projectDisplayName`, `escapeHtml`.

'disposed' : 'cancelled'` (the `disposed` flag is no longer consulted there). 'Restore was not admitted'); }`; a `failed` or `partial` restore still returns `catalog.transitive(project, ctx.signal)`, whose `dataSourceAvailable`/`errorKind` say what is on disk.    - `requireProject rejects ALL_PROJECTS` (`installed/get`, `transitive/get`, `ops/install`, `ops/bulkUpdate` with `ALL_PROJECTS` → `invalid-request`; the workflow fake never called).
   - `ops/*: a refused submission answers with the ticket's rejected outcome (Y2)` (seven submissions on a workflow fake that never resolves until released; the seventh ok response has `status: 'rejected'` and `opId` equal to the ticket's, not `'rejected'`).
   - `ops/*: the outcome is announced and invalidation follows the edit` (`announceOperation` called once with the outcome; `invalidate` with `{ kind: 'project' }` then `{ kind: 'metadata' }`).
   - `transitive/get: forceRestore runs through the queue as PackageWorkflows.restore (Y3)` (workflows.restore called with `[project]` via a `run` whose `opId` is the ticket's; `catalog.transitive` called after it; a refused ticket → `failed` error envelope).
   - `ops/bulk*: an empty batch never reaches the workflow` (`invalid-request` for the four shapes above; workflow fakes not called).
   - `installed/get answers with the final result` (`{ result: provisional, upgrade: Promise.resolve(authoritative) }` → the ok result is `authoritative`; without `upgrade` it is `result`; metadata chunks forwarded unchanged).
   - `feed reads answer unsupported while the Catalog is gated`.
   - `sources/list resolves the selected project's directory` (`catalog.sources` receives `path.dirname(selectedProject)`; with `ALL_PROJECTS` the first workspace folder; with neither → `not-found`; `failed` is `[]`).
   - `surface disposal does not cancel a queued operation (N9)` (submit on a blocked queue, `detach()`, assert `ticket.cancel` was not called and the outcome is still announced; a `cancel` envelope does cancel).
   - `resources/invalidated forwards the coordinator event unchanged` (an `InvalidationEvent` with `keys` → the event envelope's payload `toEqual` it).
   - `renderWebviewHtml: CSP per ADR-006 with font-src and a fresh nonce`: read `nonce` from `<script nonce="…"`, assert it matches `/^[A-Za-z0-9+/]{22}==$/`, assert the meta attribute equals exactly `default-src 'none'; img-src https://mock-csp-source data:; style-src https://mock-csp-source; font-src https://mock-csp-source; script-src 'nonce-${nonce}'; connect-src 'none'`, and that a second render has a different nonce.
   - `createWorkflowDeps: isCpmProject is asked once per project per operation` (two calls for the same path → one `inspect`; `onDidChange({ active: 0, pending: 0 })` → asked again; an `unsupported` inspection rejects with its message; a rejected read is not memoized).
   - `SidebarHost: a context-menu action submits a typed operation and never fabricates a request` (`window.showQuickPick` mocked to `{ action: 'uninstall' }`; `queue.submit` called with the `remove` label; no `response` envelope posted to the webview; `announceOperation` called).
   `src/host/WebviewHost.test.ts`: the seven quarry tests stay as they are (the `detach` test at `:77` still holds: reads abort, nothing is posted); add `detach aborts with reason disposed and cancel with reason cancelled` and `an error whose code is not-yet-available answers unsupported`.

### P2.10 — ⚑ composition root without feeds, transport, evaluator and mutator (2026-10-13)

Brief: plan §2.4 form, item P2.10, on `labs/2.0` @ `e4eea0f` with the quarry's `src/extension.next.ts` copied
byte-identical over the placeholder (22 type errors, 1 lint error) and nothing else written. The row names
modules that later items create (`MemoryIconCache`, `DefaultServiceIndexClient`, a transport for
`proxyConfigured()`), and the quarry root also needs the evaluator and the mutator. The brief asked what the
root contains and omits, in fifteen lettered points (omissions, credentials and policy, the cache and N45, the
commands and the SDK cache keys, `workspaceFolderFor` and `stopDirs`, session reconciliation, invalidation
forwarding, watchers, the status bar, the test seam, the shared mock, test titles, the handoff note, docs and
ledger, frozen paths). Answer, verbatim; no sentinel was created, because no frozen path changes:

`````text
DECISION: Option 2, sharpened: P2.10 composes exactly what P2.1 to P2.9 export, omits everything that needs `src/core/feeds`, `Transport`, the evaluator or the mutator, anchors the feed-side omissions with three `// REMOVAL GATE: P4.9` comments in `src/extension.next.ts`, and moves `proxyConfigured()` and the `http.*` rebuild to P4.9 (after the mandatory P4.1 proxy decision). Not Option 1: blocking P2.10 blocks the P2 gate (`node esbuild.mjs --v2` builds, plan line 238) and P2.11, and the P4.9 row already says "Wire feeds into `Catalog` + `extension.next.ts` (remove `REMOVAL GATE: P4.9`)", so the plan expects a root without feeds. Not Option 3: writing consumer-less pieces breaks D5 (breadth frozen until P4) and pre-empts P4.1, which decides what `proxyConfigured()` honours. Not `OWNER MUST DECIDE`: no `contributes` change is needed.

WHY: The brief is correct about the tree. I checked the raw copy at `/Users/mattias/github/nuiget/src/extension.next.ts:1-295` against the real exports: `src/core/cache/index.ts` exports `CACHE_POLICY` and `TieredCache` (options object, `TieredCache.ts:28-44`), not a `CachePolicy` class; `src/core/http/index.ts` exports only the ssrf helpers; `src/core/project/index.ts` has no evaluator or mutator; `DotnetSourceProvider` takes `{ runner, logger }` (`DotnetSourceProvider.ts:18-24`), `NuGetConfigReader` only `{ env }` (`NuGetConfigReader.ts:25-27`), `DefaultOutboundPolicy(mapping?)` (`:14`), `CredentialProviderOptions` wants `runner` and `lookupConfigCredential` (`DefaultCredentialProvider.ts:19-21`); `CatalogDeps` is `coordinator, inspector, evaluator?, contexts, sources, assets, discoverProjects, logger?` (`Catalog.ts:32-42`); `HostServices` has neither `output` nor `mutator` and its events are the core `Event<T>` (`services.ts:16-37`); `createWorkflowDeps` exists (`services.ts:54-87`); `package.json` contributes exactly the 12 commands the brief lists and one view (`nuiget.sidebarView`, when `nuiget.hasProjectFiles`). Governing texts: ADR-009 decisions 5 and 8 (credentials and policy belong to a project context, breadth frozen until P4), ADR-001:35 and `SessionStore.reconcile` JSDoc with the P1.7 ruling (`docs/plans/STATUS.md:492-533`: a selected-project change is a source-list change; the intermediate broadcast is acceptable pending the P2.11 harness), ADR-002:56-59 (the coordinator event forwarded unchanged), ADR-005 and `sourceDirectory` (`handlers.ts:27-36`: no guessed directory), RUN.md §4 (never stub), plan §2.1 (the item's files) and §2.3 (`contributes` is ⚑), ledger N45 (`findings-ledger.md:371`), C-state-core-5 (`:403`) and C-protocol-host-1 (`:425`). 2. (a) Omissions and who adds them. Feeds clients, `MemoryIconCache`/icon cache, `DefaultServiceIndexClient` and `SourceHealthTracker`, `startHealthMonitor` and `notifySourceHealth`, `NodeTransport`/`Transport`, `proxyConfigured()`, the `http.*` rebuild: all omitted, all P4.9 (P4.2 delivers `Transport.ts`, P4.3 the service index and health, P4.4 the icon adapter; the root wires them in P4.9). This one logger goes to every core constructor (`DefaultDotnetRunner`, `TieredCache`, `DotnetSourceProvider`, `ProjectContextProvider`, `DefaultQueryCoordinator`, `DefaultSessionStore`, `DefaultOperationQueue`, `createWorkflowDeps`, `Catalog`) and to `HostServices.logger`. []).map(f => ({ name: f.name, fsPath: f.uri.fsPath })))` (the `await` turns the Thenable into a Promise; no `Thenable` token); `workflows = new PackageWorkflows(createWorkflowDeps({ runner, catalog, inspector, queue, logger, onCommand }))` with the quarry's `onCommand` body writing through `logger`; `events = new HostEvents()`; `services: HostServices = { context, logger, session, catalog, queue, workflows, sources, onOperationCompleted: events.operationCompleted.event, onOperationProgress: queue.onProgress, onConfigChanged: events.configChanged.event, onProjectsChanged: events.projectsChanged.event, invalidate: (scope, reason) => catalog.invalidate(scope, reason), announceOperation: outcome => events.operationCompleted.fire(outcome), config: readExtensionConfig, workspaceFolderFor }` (no `vscode.Disposable.from` around core events). (f) One `refreshProjectsAndSession()` with coalescing: a module-local `inFlight: Promise<void> | undefined` and `rerun = false`; a request while one runs sets `rerun = true`; when the run settles and `rerun` is set, it clears the flag and runs once more. Body: `lastReconciledProject = session.snapshot.selectedProject` first (so the host-origin commit that `reconcile` fires cannot re-trigger); `const projects = await catalog.projects()`; `events.projectsChanged.fire(projects)`; `await vscode.commands.executeCommand('setContext', 'nuiget.hasProjectFiles', projects.length > 0)`; `const directory = sourceDirectory(services)` (import from `./host/handlers`; wrap in try) and `const sources = await catalog.sources(directory)`; on success remember `lastListing = { directory, urls }` and `await session.reconcile(projects, enabledUrls)`. On a failed listing or a `not-found` from `sourceDirectory`: never pass `[]`; use `lastListing.urls` if `lastListing.directory === directory`, else `session.snapshot.selectedSource === 'all' ? [] : [session.snapshot.selectedSource]` (keeps the selection without claiming knowledge), log a warning, still call `reconcile` with the real `projects` (a deleted project must not stay selected). Triggers: at the end of `activateWith` (not awaited); from `coordinator.onDidInvalidate` when `event.scope.kind` is `workspace`, `project` or `sources` (not `package`, not `metadata`: they reach neither `projects` nor `sources:*`, `scopes.ts:15-17`); from `session.onDidChange` when `snapshot.selectedProject !== lastReconciledProject`. `afterSourceMutation` (`handlers.ts:307-312`) reconciles too; the duplicate is coalesced and idempotent. The intermediate broadcast between `apply` and `reconcile` stays acceptable; P2.11 proves convergence (P1.7 ruling).

9. (g) Forward at event time: `coordinator.onDidInvalidate(event => { sidebar.notifyInvalidated(event); MainPanelHost.instance?.notifyInvalidated(event); if (...) requestRefresh(); })`; delete the `hosts` array and `trackMain`; `openMain = options => MainPanelHost.createOrShow(services, options)`.

One helper `projectFileIn(directory): Promise<string | undefined>` using `fs.promises.readdir` (`import * as fs from 'fs'`), used by the assets watcher (`path.dirname(path.dirname(assetsPath))`) and the lock-file watcher (`path.dirname(lockPath)`); the timer callback becomes async; no `require`. Keep the `queue.isBusy` → `await queue.waitIdle()` re-arm. `onDidChangeConfiguration`: keep the `nuiget` / `workbench.tree.indent` branch firing `events.configChanged.fire(readExtensionConfig())`; the `http` branch is gate (iii) of step 2.

(i) Status bar in the root: `const statusBar = vscode.window.createStatusBarItem(vscode.StatusBarAlignment.Left, 100)`; `queue.onDidChange(({ active, pending }) => { if (active + pending === 0) { statusBar.hide(); return; } statusBar.text = pending > 0 ? \`$(sync~spin) nUIget: ${active} running, ${pending} queued\` : '$(sync~spin) nUIget: operation running'; statusBar.show(); })`; push the item and the listener to subscriptions.

No `vi.mock` of `./core/cli`: a partial barrel mock is fragile under `tsconfig.vitest.json` and would bypass the real hydrate/sweep/flush the tests check. The test writes its own small fake `SpawnFunction` (an `EventEmitter` child that answers `dotnet --version` and `dotnet nuget list source --format detailed` per `cwd`; `src/core/cli/DotnetRunner.test.ts:66-110` shows the shape, it is not exported and is not this item's file to export).

after `services.session.apply({ selectedProject: second }, 'sidebar')` and settling, `spawn` saw `nuget list source` with the second directory and `snapshot.selectedSource === 'all'`); `a failed source listing keeps the selected source` (listing exits 1 → `selectedSource` unchanged, `reconcile` still ran: a persisted project that no longer exists is replaced); `nuiget.refreshPackages`; both `postMessage` mocks receive an event envelope with `type 'resources/invalidated'` whose payload has `keys` containing `{ key: 'projects', generation }`); `a disposed main panel receives no further events`; `nuiget.clearHttpCache clears the network cache and invalidates the workspace` (`spawn` saw `nuget locals http-cache --clear`); `nuiget.clearSdkVersionCache clears the runner cache and invalidates the workspace`;
