### P5.4 — stop-and-ask: multi-selection, All Projects grouping, scope and the harness scenario (2026-10-14)

Brief: plan §2.4 form, item P5.4, on `labs/2.0` @ `9000208`. Trigger: plan §2.1 (multi-selection for the bulk
toolbar and the All Projects grouping need `src/webview/shared/primitives/Listbox.tsx`, which neither the row nor the
P3.6 ruling names) and the row's harness gate `bulk remove partial failure reconciles`, a scenario that does not
exist yet. Options given unpicked: A/B/C for multi-selection, D/E/F for grouping, G/H for scope. The brief said
nothing of P5.4 was written; the N61 change was in the working tree by the time the subagent read it, which its
correction records. Answer, verbatim; no sentinel, no frozen path:

`````text
CORRECTION TO THE BRIEF (checked first, per the agent definition): the tree is not "nothing of P5.4 written yet". `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` on `labs/2.0` @ `9000208` lists five modified, uncommitted files: `src/webview/shared/store/ops.tsx` (+100/-?), `src/webview/shared/store/ops.test.tsx` (+64), `src/webview/sidebar-next/InstalledSection.tsx` (-27 net), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (19 lines) and `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (one gotcha row). HEAD's `ops.tsx` has no `asked`/`named`/`settled` action and no `onChunk` (`git show HEAD:src/webview/shared/store/ops.tsx | grep -c "type: 'asked'\|onChunk"` → 0) and HEAD's `InstalledSection.tsx` still has the pending set (7 matches); the working tree has the N61 move done exactly as the P5.4 row prescribes (`run()` records from the call via `touchedBy`, re-keys on the first chunk, removes both entries on settle; the two named test titles `a row is busy from the call to run until the request settles` and `a request without an answer leaves nothing busy` plus two more; the pending set and `busyStatus`'s first branch deleted; `SidebarView.test.tsx` untouched, so every title is kept), the ARCHITECTURE "queued" sentence of P3 review non-blocking 1 already rewritten (`ARCHITECTURE.md:330-334`), and the gotcha row "An operation that waits in the queue has no `ops/progress`" rewritten. One leftover in that diff: the gotcha row "Removing a row when an operation succeeds" (`copilot-instructions.md:196`) still says "A row is busy from the click (the section's own pending set)". So commit (a) below is "gate and commit what is there", not "write N61". The GATE STATUS in the brief describes HEAD, not the working tree; run the three baseline gates on the working tree before anything else.

DECISION: Multi-selection: option A, as an additive `Listbox` contract (single mode unchanged; multi mode only when `selectedIds` is passed: `aria-multiselectable`, `aria-selected` = the set, `selectedId` becomes `aria-current`, Space / Ctrl+click (Cmd+click on macOS) / click on an `aria-hidden` check mark inside the option toggle; no Shift range, no Ctrl+A). Grouping: option D (one `SectionHeader` + one `Listbox` per project from the `allProjects/installed` chunks; the bulk toolbar aggregates the per-list sets into `projectRemovals`); per-workspace-folder headings, "Unsupported" rows incl. `sdk-too-old`, and the all-projects transitive aggregate stay P5.9's and P5.4 writes that on P5.9's tracker row. Scope: option G sharpened by the P3.2 precedent: one tracker row P5.4, three green commits in a fixed order (a: N61 as it is in the tree; b: `Listbox`/`Row` multi-selection, the lifted row rules, the `transitive` and `allProjects` slices, the harness scenario; c: the Installed feature of the main panel with the transitive section, the bulk toolbar and dialog, the ExTester test, docs), each pushed with green CI before the next; no `P5.4a`/`P5.4b` rows. Files beyond the P3.6-named ones are approved below. Harness scenario named, stepped and asserted under DO 6; it needs no change to `harness.ts` or `fakeDotnet.ts`. Not `OWNER MUST DECIDE`: nothing frozen, nothing under `contributes`, the owner approved §3/§5/§8 of the interaction model and the plan row text; the P3.2 ruling (`docs/plans/STATUS.md`, "P3.2 — ⚑ ruling", step 4) already reserved multi-selection for "P5's bulk toolbar" and asked that `selectedId` be designed "so a `selectedIds` set is additive".

WHY: The brief is right about the committed tree in every other point I checked (`Listbox.tsx:20,47,57,205` single `selectedId`; `Row.tsx:79` `aria-selected`; `messages.ts:127,159,161`; `handlers.ts:114-123,134-143,286,379,381`; `Catalog.ts:196-197` and `handlers.ts:283` for `transitive/metadata`; `scenarios.test.ts` has 9 scenarios in `two-surface harness`, none for bulk remove; `fakeDotnet.ts:161` `fail` unused). Option B contradicts §8's `uninstall-selected-button` (interaction model line 133) and §5's "Dialog listing the packages": "uninstall everything shown" is not a selection. Option C and H create rows; the P3.2 ruling ("one tracker row, two green commits in a fixed order … not a split and not a second row") and the P3.6 ruling (which split P5.12 only because its two halves have different ⚑ status) are the precedents, and the harness gate of the row is reached in commit b, so an interrupted session loses the least. ADR-008 line 44 ("Accessibility is a property of primitives, not of each feature") puts multi-selection into `Listbox`, not into the feature; axe-core 4.12.1 lists `aria-current` among `globalAttributes` (`node_modules/axe-core/axe.js:21775`, `:14179-14183` values incl. `true`), so an `option` may carry it, and the P3.2 ruling's WHY already confirmed `aria-multiselectable` is allowed on `listbox`; a check mark that is a text-only `aria-hidden` span inside the option keeps the "nothing but text inside the option" rule and `nested-interactive` passes (`CheckIcon` exists, `icons.tsx:67`). Option D stays inside §5's "one tab stop per list" and gives "row actions apply to that project" (§3) for free, needs no virtualizer change (an unconstrained `.nuiget-listbox` grows to `--nuiget-virtual-total` and the real virtualizer renders every row, which is what 1.x does for All Projects), and composes with P5.9's folder headings (`SectionHeader` has a `level` prop); options E (no visible groups) and F (group rows in a virtualized listbox, the active-row and focus logic of `Listbox.tsx:55-69,95-119` would have to skip them) cost more and give less. P5.9's row text ("per-folder grouping, unsupported rows incl. `sdk-too-old`", gate `late allProjects chunk after switching is discarded`) owns the rest of §3. The `allProjects` slice cannot be a `useResource`: `Catalog.ts:216` ("A fan-out is not a resource (ADR-009): it reports no generation of its own") and `resourceKeys.allProjectsInstalled()` is never in `keys`, so the slice follows the all-projects branch of `FakeWebview.ts:218-222,231` (re-request when `keys` names `projects` or any `installed:` key; chunks of an earlier request recorded, not applied); `transitive/get` is a resource with a generation (`Catalog.ts:192-193`, `handlers.ts:279-281`), so the `transitive` slice is a `useResource` on `resourceKeys.transitive(path)`. `PackageWorkflows.ts:119-123,164-180,273-299,363-373`: a failed `package remove` does not stop the others, the project is restored once when any edit succeeded, and `bulkStatus` yields `partial`; `fakeDotnet.ts:121-125` answers a `fail` before the on-disk edit, so the failed package stays in the file; `handlers.ts:196-227` invalidates the project after the outcome, which is what both surfaces reconcile from. `orderPackages` (`PackageWorkflows.ts:324-335`) reads `obj/project.assets.json`, which the scratch tree lacks, and falls through its catch to the given order, so the order of the three removes is not pinned. `whyNotRemovable`/`shownVersion` (`InstalledSection.tsx:21-50`) and `describeOutcome` (`sidebar-next/outcomes.ts`) are needed by both surfaces; ADR-008 line 16-18 puts shared source under `webview/shared`, and `main` must not import from `sidebar-next`.

Tests added to `Listbox.test.tsx`: `a multi-selectable listbox marks the rows of the set selected and the current row aria-current`, `Space toggles the active row; Ctrl+click, Cmd+click and a click on the check mark do the same; a plain click selects`, `Space on a disabled option toggles nothing, and Space without a selection set is not handled`, `a multi-selectable listbox with a current row and check marks has no axe violations` (also assert `axeRuleOutcome(results, 'aria-allowed-attr')` is `'passes'`). Added to `Row.test.tsx`: `in a multi-selectable list the option shows a decorative check mark and is aria-current when it is the current row`. `docs/ux/interaction-model.md` §5, append to the keyboard paragraph: "A list that offers bulk actions is `aria-multiselectable`: Space toggles whether the active row is selected for them (Ctrl+click, Cmd+click on macOS, or a click on the row's check mark does the same), the rows selected for them are `aria-selected`, and the row whose details are shown is `aria-current`. Select all selects every row the action applies to; the confirmation dialog lists exactly those rows." §8: no new id (`select-all-button`, `uninstall-selected-button`, `transitive-section`, `transitive-restore-banner`, `installed-list`, `confirm-dialog*` exist); change the `package-row-<id>` cell to end "(read `aria-selected` there; in a multi-selectable list `aria-current` marks the current row)".

New slice `src/webview/shared/store/transitive.tsx` (`TransitiveProvider`, `useTransitive()`): state `{ projectPath: string | null; enabled: boolean; status; result: TransitiveResult | undefined; metadata: ReadonlyMap<string, PackageMetadataLite>; error; revalidating; enable(): void; restore(): void; refresh(): void }`; the key is `resourceKeys.transitive(committed.selectedProject)` only while `enabled` (set once on the first expand of the section, reset on project change) and `null` otherwise; the requester calls `transitive/get { projectPath, forceRestore }` where `forceRestore` is a ref set by `restore()` for exactly the next request, then `refresh()`; after a result with `frameworks`, ask `transitive/metadata` for the distinct `{ id, version }` pairs and keep the answer by lower-cased `id@version`; an error response to that request, `unsupported` included, is an empty map and no error of the slice. Tests `transitive.test.tsx`: `asks nothing until it is enabled, then transitive/get for the committed project`, `an invalidation of the transitive key asks again and keeps the sections meanwhile` (R3), `late response is discarded` (R4), `restore asks transitive/get with forceRestore once and the rows stay until the answer`, `transitive/metadata answering unsupported is no metadata, and the rows come from transitive/get alone` (the P3 review non-blocking 6 test), `another project resets the section to collapsed and asks nothing`. New slice `src/webview/shared/store/allProjects.tsx` (`AllProjectsProvider`, `useAllProjects()`): active only while `useSession().committed?.selectedProject === ALL_PROJECTS`; state `{ active: boolean; status: 'idle' | 'loading' | 'ready' | 'error'; projects: Project[] | undefined (from the start chunk); chunks: ReadonlyMap<string, ProjectInstalledChunk> (by projectPath as sent, latest chunk per project wins); metadata: ReadonlyMap<string, ReadonlyMap<string, PackageMetadataLite>> (by project, then lower-cased id); errored; error; revalidating; refresh(): void }`; it is not a `useResource`: it subscribes to `resources/invalidated` itself and asks again when `event.keys` names `resourceKeys.projects()` or any key starting with `installed:` (FakeWebview R3, `FakeWebview.ts:218-222`), keeps the previous chunks while the new request runs (no blanking, interaction model §4), numbers its requests and applies chunks and the response of the latest only (R4; `FakeWebview.ts:231`), and compares no generation (ADR-009, `Catalog.ts:216`). Tests `allProjects.test.tsx`: `asks allProjects/installed in all-projects mode only and collects the project chunks by path`, `asks again when an invalidation names projects or any installed key, and keeps the chunks meanwhile`, `chunks and the response of an earlier request are recorded and not applied`, `an errored project is kept as its error, a later chunk for the same project replaces it`, `leaving all-projects mode drops the state`. `StoreProvider.tsx` mounts both (`TransitiveProvider` inside `InstalledProvider`, `AllProjectsProvider` beside it, both above `OpsProvider`); `StoreProvider.test.tsx` gains `mounts the transitive and allProjects slices`. The harness scenario, in `src/test/harness/scenarios.test.ts` inside `describe('two-surface harness')`, title starting with the row's phrase verbatim: `it('bulk remove partial failure reconciles: the failed package stays, the others go, both surfaces show the list on disk', …)`. No change to `harness.ts` or `fakeDotnet.ts`: `operate` accepts `ops/bulkRemove` (`harness.ts:24,265`), `fail` and `dotnetCalls.packageRemove(id)` exist (`fakeDotnet.ts:69-70,161`). Tree: `{ 'A/A.csproj': projectXml(['Serilog', '3.0.0'], ['Moq', '4.20.0'], ['Newtonsoft.Json', '13.0.1']), 'B/B.csproj': projectXml(['Newtonsoft.Json', '13.0.1']) }`, `selectedProject: 'A/A.csproj'`. Steps: (i) `h = await createHarness(…)`; `const [A, B] = [h.path('A/A.csproj'), h.path('B/B.csproj')]`; resolve `sidebar` and `main`; `await h.expectState({ selectedProject: A, projects: [A, B], installed: ['Serilog@3.0.0', 'Moq@4.20.0', 'Newtonsoft.Json@13.0.1'] })` (file order; if the inspector orders otherwise the literal list changes, the property does not). (ii) `const failing = h.dotnet.fail(dotnetCalls.packageRemove('Moq'), { code: 1, stderr: 'error: Moq could not be removed (https://user:secret@feed.example/v3/index.json)' })`; `const restore = h.dotnet.hold(dotnetCalls.restore())`; `const marks = new Map([sidebar, main].map(w => [w, w.received.length]))`. (iii) `const request = h.operate('main', 'ops/bulkRemove', { projectPath: A, packages: ['Serilog', 'Moq', 'Newtonsoft.Json'] })`; `await h.settle(() => expect(restore.seen).toBe(1))`. Assert mid-way: `main.chunksFor<'ops/bulkRemove'>(request.id)[0]` matches `{ status: 'queued' }`; the chunks contain one `{ status: 'running', kind: 'remove', projectPaths: [A], packageIds: ['Serilog', 'Moq', 'Newtonsoft.Json'] }` (the opening report of `editSequence`) and one with `status: 'restoring'`; the file on disk (`fs.readFileSync(A, 'utf8')`) does not contain `Serilog` or `Newtonsoft.Json` and still contains `Moq`; `packagesOf(main.installed)` and `packagesOf(sidebar.installed)` still equal the original three (nothing optimistic, interaction model §4 "Partial / failed op"). (iv) `restore.release()`; `const outcome = resultOf(await request.response)`. Assert: `outcome.status === 'partial'`; `[...outcome.results].sort((a, b) => a.packageId!.localeCompare(b.packageId!)).map(r => [r.packageId, r.kind, r.status])` equals `[['Moq', 'remove', 'failed'], ['Newtonsoft.Json', 'remove', 'completed'], ['Serilog', 'remove', 'completed']]` (sorted, because the order of the three removes is not pinned: `orderPackages` falls through without an assets file); the Moq result has `edit.ok === false`, a `message` that contains `Moq` and does not contain `secret`; `outcome.restores` equals `[{ projectPath: A, ok: true }]`; `outcome.opId` matches the UUID pattern. (v) `await h.expectState({ selectedProject: A, projects: [A, B], installed: ['Moq@4.20.0'] })` (the reconciliation: the list is what is on disk, on both surfaces; `expectState` also asserts one response per request and an idle queue). Then for each of `[sidebar, main]`: `webview.completed().filter(o => o.opId === outcome.opId).map(statusOf)` equals `['partial']`; the `resources/invalidated` events since its mark include one with `reason: 'operation'` whose `keys` contain `resourceKeys.installed(A)` and whose `scope` is `{ kind: 'project', projectPaths: [A] }`; `expectOneResponsePerRequest(webview)`. `h.dotnet.count(dotnetCalls.packageRemove())` is 3 and `h.dotnet.count(dotnetCalls.restore())` is 1 (one restore for the project, not one per edit). (vi) Recovery tail: `failing()`; `const again = resultOf(await h.operate('sidebar', 'ops/bulkRemove', { projectPath: A, packages: ['Moq'] }).response)`; `again.status === 'completed'`; `await h.expectState({ selectedProject: A, projects: [A, B], installed: [] })`; `h.dotnet.count(dotnetCalls.restore())` is 2. Fails if a surface removes Moq by guessing, if a failed edit stops the batch, if the batch is `failed` or `completed` instead of `partial`, if the stderr credential reaches the outcome, or if the project is restored per edit. Behaviour: the current row is local `useState<string | null>` (`selectedId`), set by click and Enter (`onSelect`, `onActivate`), shown as `aria-current`; the bulk set is `useState<ReadonlyMap<string, ReadonlySet<string>>>` keyed by project path exactly as the host sent it; `onToggleSelected` flips a row only when `whyNotRemovable(entry, result) === undefined` and otherwise `announce`s the obstacle; the `Toolbar` (`label="Bulk actions"`) above the lists holds `select-all-button` ("Select all", `aria-pressed` when everything removable is selected; a second press clears) and `uninstall-selected-button` ("Uninstall selected (N)", disabled at 0); Confirm calls `run('ops/bulkRemove', { projectPath, packages })` in single-project mode and `run('ops/bulkRemoveAllProjects', { projectRemovals: [{ projectPath, projectName, packages }] })` in All Projects, closes the dialog at once, removes the sent ids from the set, and relies on the ops slice for the busy rows (N61; `isPackageBusy(projectPath, id)`), on the store for the new list (`resources/invalidated` → `installed/get` / `allProjects/installed`), and on the outcome announcement for the result; it deletes nothing from a list itself. `Delete`/`onDelete` on a single row stays `ops/remove` (no confirmation, §5). Filtering: `filter` by id substring (case-insensitive) as the sidebar; `vulnerableOnly` keeps rows whose metadata (`installed.metadata`, chunk `kind: 'metadata'`) has `vulnerabilities.length > 0`, and when it is on and no row has metadata at all the `EmptyState` says "Vulnerability data is not available for this project yet" (empty is not unknown), else "No vulnerable packages". Hints: single list "Space selects the package for a bulk action. Enter shows its details. Delete uninstalls it. Shift+F10 opens the actions menu." Outcomes announced once per surface: `MainShell.tsx` gains, as `SidebarView.tsx` has, one effect over `useOps().last` that calls `announce(describeOutcome(last.outcome))` when `last.sequence` changes (shell stays far below 300 lines; one test in `MainShell.test.tsx`: `announces the outcome of an operation of either surface once`). Wiring: `src/webview/main/index.tsx` renders `<MainShell installed={view => <InstalledTab {...view} />} />`; `index.test.tsx` rewritten in place (P5.3 precedent, titles kept where they still hold). Feature tests `InstalledTab.test.tsx` with `createFakeHost()` and `installFakeLayout()`: `lists the committed project's packages as a multi-selectable list with the filter applied`, `select all selects the removable rows only and the toolbar counts them`, `the dialog lists the selected packages; Escape and Cancel send nothing`, `Confirm asks ops/bulkRemove with exactly the listed ids, closes the dialog, and the rows are busy until the request settles`, `a partial outcome leaves the failed row where the host's list has it and is announced`, `All Projects: one section per project, selections across them become ops/bulkRemoveAllProjects with the project names`, `the transitive section asks on first expand, shows the restore banner when the assets file is missing, and Restore asks with forceRestore`, `@vulnerable shows the vulnerable rows, or says that the data is not there yet`, `has no axe violations with the dialog open and with All Projects sections` (`axe(document.body)`).

### P4.1 — ⚑ proxy and certificate matrix before Transport (2026-10-14)

Brief: plan §2.4 form, item P4.1, on `labs/2.0` @ `18fe2cd` with nothing written. It pasted the facts F1-F8 that a
research subagent of the session read at VS Code 1.85.0 / `@vscode/proxy-agent` 0.18.2 and at the current release
(with their citations), the quarry's `Transport.ts` and `proxyConfigured()` excerpts, ADR-005's `Transport` bullet,
and twelve options unpicked (HTTP/1.1 path A/B/C, HTTP/2 D/E/F, vendoring G/H, settings I/J, contract text K/L).
Answer, verbatim:

`````text
DECISION: HTTP/1.1 = A (no agent of our own; the host patch does the proxying); HTTP/2 = E sharpened (allow-listed origins only, gated by the injected predicate `http.proxySupport !== 'off' && (http.proxy set || HTTPS_PROXY/https_proxy/HTTP_PROXY/http_proxy set)`, with a connection-level failure downgrading the origin to HTTP/1.1 for the lifetime of the `Transport` instance instead of the quarry's 60 s cooldown); vendoring = G (none); settings = J (`http.proxyStrictSSL` and `http.proxyAuthorization` are stated as not applied, never emulated, certificate verification never disabled); contract text = K (amend ADR-005 and write `docs/adr/005-proxy-matrix.md` as a supplement; `src/core/contracts.ts` is not touched). Nothing in this item is OWNER MUST DECIDE: no `contributes` change, no dependency added, no release or scope question; the plan row itself delegates the vendoring decision to P4.1 with advisor sign-off.

WHY: The brief is wrong about the tree in one place, and it matters: `src/core/contracts.ts:254-258` does not mention `http.proxyStrictSSL`. It says "Honours VS Code proxy settings by routing HTTP/1.1 through Node's `https` module (which the extension host patches for proxies); HTTP/2 is used only when no proxy is configured", which stays true under this ruling, so option L dissolves and D6 stays narrow (P4.1 is named for `docs/adr/**` only, plan line 50). The brief is right about `docs/adr/005-execution-context-and-outbound-policy.md:30-36` (names `http.proxyStrictSSL`, and the P1.1b diff review at `docs/plans/STATUS.md:740` already left the dropped "falls back to HTTP/1.1" clause to P4.1), about the three `REMOVAL GATE: P4.9` anchors in `src/extension.next.ts:72,205,274`, about the P4.2/P4.9 tracker notes (`docs/plans/STATUS.md:64,71`) and about `src/core/http` holding only `ssrf.ts`, `index.ts`, tests and the P1.9 note. On the facts F1-F8 I have no outside access and could not verify them; they are consistent with each other and with the GitLab and C# extension behaviour the subagent cites, and the ruling rests on two of them (F2, F4), which DO step 3 makes the session re-read at the pinned tags before writing. Given F4, the choice on the HTTP/1.1 path is decided by `http.proxySupport: on`, not by keep-alive: under `override` (the default) a caller-supplied agent is discarded anyway, and under `on` a defined agent bypasses the proxy entirely, which is a correctness failure for exactly the corporate users the plan's §7 risk row ("Transport/proxy regresses corporate users | P4.1 before P4.2; HTTP/2 only without proxy", plan line 358) protects; passing no agent is correct in all four modes and the only cost is keep-alive on Node 18 hosts (D10 baseline), which Node 19 and later hosts restore through the global agent. Given F3, the HTTP/2 predicate cannot see an operating-system or PAC proxy or a TLS-intercepting CA, so the h2→h1 fallback is the real safety net and must be sticky: a 60 s cooldown against a connect that times out under a dropping firewall is a recurring stall. Dropping HTTP/2 (option D) would reopen the plan's own mitigation and the P4.2 row's test list ("h2→h1 fallback", plan line 258), so it is not taken. Vendoring an agent is reached only when the user has set `http.proxySupport: off`, where honouring `http.proxy` would contradict the user's own setting, is replaced by the host under `override` (F4), loses PAC, OS proxy, Kerberos and OS certificates (F7), and adds a dependency with Node 18 version ceilings; so none. Emulating `http.proxyStrictSSL` would mean `rejectUnauthorized: false` from a setting VS Code itself scopes to its own request service (F2), which `.github/instructions/security-and-owasp.instructions.md` forbids; the dotnet CLI does not read VS Code settings either, so J loses no parity.

Re-verify the two load-bearing facts at the pinned sources before writing, and record tag, path, line and the date in the matrix: F2 (`https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/blob/1.85.0/src/vs/platform/request/node/requestService.ts` lines 73-74 read `proxyStrictSSL`/`proxyAuthorization`; `https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/blob/1.85.0/src/vs/workbench/api/node/proxyResolver.ts` lines 36-39 and 88-96 do not) and F4 (`https://github.com/microsoft/vscode-proxy-agent/blob/v0.18.2/src/index.ts` lines 319-353, `originalAgent: undefined` under `override`, caller's agent kept under `fallback`, bypass under `on` with a defined agent; `src/agent.ts` lines 104-108, `https.globalAgent` on DIRECT). Also verify the subagent's open item from the Node 19.0.0 release notes ("HTTP(S)/1.1 KeepAlive by default") and, for the matrix's host-Node column, the VS Code release that moved the extension host to Node 20 (expected 1.92, Electron 30; read `https://code.visualstudio.com/updates/v1_92`). If a re-read contradicts F2 or F4, stop: do not write the ADR, send a new brief with the contradicting text (universal trigger: evidence differs).

Amend `docs/adr/005-execution-context-and-outbound-policy.md` in the `dd1ed73` form: header `Status: accepted · Date: 2026-10-10 · Amended: 2026-10-12 (…) · Amended: 2026-10-14 (plan item P4.1: proxy and certificate matrix, no vendored agent)`; add to Context the paragraph "The amendment of 2026-10-14 records plan item P4.1: how the VS Code extension host treats an extension's `https` and `http2` requests (read at VS Code 1.85.0 with `@vscode/proxy-agent` 0.18.2, and at the current release), and the configuration matrix in `005-proxy-matrix.md`."; replace the `Transport` bullet (lines 30-36) with exactly: "`Transport` opens HTTP/1.1 connections through Node's `http`/`https` request functions **without an agent of its own**, so that the extension host's proxy patch applies: under `http.proxySupport` `override` (the default), `on` and `fallback` the host resolves the proxy for every request (`http.proxy`, then the `https_proxy`/`http_proxy` environment, then the operating-system or PAC configuration), authenticates to it (credentials in the `http.proxy` URL, Kerberos/Negotiate, the host's own prompts) and adds the operating-system certificate store when `http.systemCertificates` is on. VS Code does not apply `http.proxyStrictSSL` or `http.proxyAuthorization` to extension requests, and `Transport` does not emulate them; certificate verification is never disabled. HTTP/2 (`http2.connect`) is outside the host patch (no proxy, no operating-system certificates): it is used only for allow-listed origins (default `https://api.nuget.org`) and only while the injected proxy predicate is false; the predicate is true when `http.proxySupport` is not `off` and `http.proxy` or a proxy environment variable is set. A proxy the predicate cannot see (operating-system or PAC) and a TLS-intercepting proxy are handled by the fallback: a connection-level failure (DNS, connect, TLS handshake) downgrades the origin to HTTP/1.1 for the lifetime of the `Transport` instance; the composition root resets the transport when an `http.*` setting changes. No proxy agent is vendored; neither `undici` nor the global `fetch` is used. One redirect/SSRF implementation; same-origin-only auth forwarding; a redirect target whose hostname is not an IP literal is re-validated by resolved address while the predicate is false and refused with `redirect-blocked` when it does not resolve; while the predicate is true the proxy resolves names and the string checks alone apply. The supported configuration matrix is `005-proxy-matrix.md` (supplement, plan item P4.1)."; add to Consequences: "Corporate proxies work exactly as far as VS Code's own proxy support reaches an extension (`005-proxy-matrix.md`); nUIget contributes no proxy or certificate setting of its own."

what is never patched: `http2`, and global `fetch` before 1.96); section "Matrix" as one table with columns `Setting / value | VS Code range | https.request from nUIget (no agent) | http2.connect from nUIget | nUIget behaviour`, with at least these rows: `http.proxySupport: override` (default), `on`, `fallback`, `off`;
